1 tool

I dissected the real v1.0.1 source line by line and compared it against the current v2 code. I now understand exactly why v1.0.1 worked on your Poco F3 and why v2 is broken on all three fronts. Here is the complete report. No code changed — I'm waiting for your go.

▪ FULL ANALYSIS — v1.0.1 (working) vs current v2 (broken)
A. The fundamental architecture difference
v1.0.1 (worked great)	v2.3.x (current, broken)
UI	Daemon-served WebUI at 127.0.0.1:9190	Kotlin Compose app (libsu RootService)
Status source	Daemon is single source of truth (WebUI polls daemon HTTP API)	App spawns root shell sh ovpn.service status every 2s via libsu binder
SSH transport	Adaptive SSH connection POOL (size 8)	Single SSH connection
TCP tuning	SetNoDelay(true), adaptive 128–512 KB buffers (pooled)	No SetNoDelay, fixed 128 KB buffer
VPN chain	tun2socks + OpenVPN over SOCKS, MASQUERADE, fwmark	OpenVPN over SOCKS, uidrange routing, forced MTU 1280
Hotspot	ip_forward=1 + FORWARD -j ACCEPT	(v2.3.13) ip_forward + route_localnet, no FORWARD ACCEPT
Network loss	Watchdog → pause/standby → auto-resume	Daemon hammers reconnect for 15 min, never parks
This one table explains everything. The v2 rewrite dropped three things that made v1.0.1 good: the SSH pool, the network-standby watchdog, and the correct hotspot forwarding rules — and it added a fragile libsu status path plus throughput-hurting OpenVPN flags.

B. Root causes of each bug
1️⃣ VPN Chain tab stuck "Off" + no exit IP
v1.0.1: the WebUI asks the daemon (GET /api/v1/vpnchain/status); the daemon runs vpnchain.sh status which returns JSON read from state.json. The WebUI lives in the same trust domain as the daemon — always reliable.

v2: the app reads VPN-chain status only through rootBinder?.vpnChainStatus() → Shell.cmd("sh ovpn.service status") over libsu's RootService.

If the RootService binder isn't connected (rootBinder == null — common: libsu root service is separate from the app's main shell and frequently isn't bound), vpnChainStatus() returns null → "stopped" → tab shows Off forever, even though OpenVPN is fully connected.
The Home tab keeps working because it reads the daemon over the Unix socket / HTTP (a totally different, reliable channel). That's why Home is fine but VPN Chain is stuck — they use different status pipes.
Exit IP (fetchChainExitIp → https://ipinfo.io) only runs after state becomes Connected. Since state never flips to Connected, exit IP stays "—". Also, it's fetched from the app's own process, which is fragile.
➡️ This is architectural, not a one-line bug. My v2.3.13 "detach the watcher" fix removed one failure mode but the status is still read through the flaky libsu shell. The correct fix is to make the daemon own VPN-chain state and report it in the same status snapshot the app already reads reliably for Home — exactly like v1.0.1.

2️⃣ Hotspot not shared
v1.0.1 (works) — in iptables.go, hotspot mode does:

PREROUTING -i wlan+/ap+/rndis+/... -p tcp -j SSHC_PREROUTING   (REDIRECT → transparent port)
sysctl net.ipv4.ip_forward=1
iptables -I FORWARD -j ACCEPT          ← THE CRITICAL LINE
v2 (broken): start_redirect's hotspot block creates the REDIRECT jump, and my v2.3.13 added ip_forward=1 + route_localnet, but never adds FORWARD -j ACCEPT. On Android, netd's FORWARD chain default-drops tethered packets, so the client traffic dies. route_localnet was a red herring — REDIRECT in PREROUTING rewrites to the interface IP, not loopback, so the transparent listener on 0.0.0.0:9799 already catches it. The missing piece vs the known-good build is FORWARD -j ACCEPT (+ ip_forward).

3️⃣ Speed extremely low (even when chained)
The dominant cause is the architecture change, not the chain itself:

v1.0.1 used an adaptive pool of 8 SSH connections (SSHPool, pool.Healthy()/Size()), spreading app streams across many TCP carriers. v2 funnels every stream through ONE SSH TCP connection → single congestion window + cipher stream + head-of-line blocking = throughput collapse. The channel_pool/channel_pool_size=8 settings still exist in v2's config.go but are parsed and never used — the pool was deleted in the rewrite.
v1.0.1 sets TCP_NODELAY on sockets; v2 doesn't → Nagle adds latency to the interactive chain.
v2 forces OpenVPN --tun-mtu 1280 --mssfix 1200 --sndbuf/--rcvbuf 524288; v1.0.1 uses OpenVPN defaults (MTU 1500). The forced low MTU shrinks every packet ~15% and the OpenVPN log literally warns about the inconsistency; the oversized buffers worsen bufferbloat over TCP-over-TCP.
➡️ Restoring the SSH connection pool is the big win; TCP_NODELAY and dropping the MTU/buffer overrides are smaller, additive wins.

C. New feature you asked for — "data off → stop & standby, resume when network returns"
v1.0.1 already had this fully built, and v2 lost it:

sshcustom_watchdog.sh polls has_route() (ip route get 1.1.1.1) every ~10s.
Route lost (after 2 misses) → sshcustom.sh network-pause: kills the daemon, cleans iptables, writes network_paused marker, sets module.prop to 🟡 "paused, waiting for network". No background reconnect hammering.
Route returns → network-resume: restarts daemon, sets ○ running.
The daemon also cooperates: it sets internal state PAUSED_NO_NETWORK and a 10s routeTicker, and module.prop shows 🟡 standby.
v2 has none of this. Its tunnelLoop just retries dialing with backoff for 15 minutes while holding iptables (fail-closed) — i.e. it keeps running in the background, exactly what you don't want.

➡️ Plan: port v1.0.1's standby model into v2 — daemon detects no usable route → enters STANDBY (stops dialing, tears down listeners/rules, sets 🟡), and auto-resumes when a default route reappears.

D. Implementation plan (proposed v2.4.0 — I'll wait for your go)
Fix 1 — VPN-chain state owned by the daemon (kills the "Off" bug for good)

Daemon tracks chain state from the vpnchain.active marker + openvpn liveness + the Windscribe exit IP, and adds vpnchain_state + vpnchain_exit_ip to the status snapshot (unix.go/main.go).
App reads VPN-chain state from the same daemon status flow as Home (DaemonRepository.statusFlow), not from libsu. Remove the per-2s sh ovpn.service status polling entirely.
Files: daemon/.../api/unix.go, daemon/cmd/sshcustomd/main.go, MainViewModel.kt, VpnChainScreen.kt, Repository.kt.
Fix 2 — Hotspot (match v1.0.1 exactly)

In ssh.iptables hotspot block: keep ip_forward=1, add iptables -I FORWARD -j ACCEPT, drop the unnecessary route_localnet. Add matching cleanup in the disable path + a net_clean-style sweep.
File: module/scripts/ssh.iptables.
Fix 3 — Speed: restore the SSH connection pool + TCP_NODELAY + sane OpenVPN flags

Reintroduce an SSH connection pool (size from channel_pool_size, default 8) in the daemon; SOCKS/transparent pick a healthy client per connection. This is the meaningful refactor and the biggest win.
Add SetNoDelay(true) on accepted conns in socks5.go/transparent.go.
In ovpn.service: remove --tun-mtu 1280 --mssfix 1200 --sndbuf/--rcvbuf (use OpenVPN defaults like v1.0.1).
Files: daemon/.../ssh/client.go (or new pool.go), daemon/cmd/sshcustomd/main.go, proxy/socks5.go, proxy/transparent.go, module/scripts/ovpn.service.
Fix 4 — Network standby on data-off

Add route-awareness to the daemon tunnelLoop: no usable default route → enter STANDBY (stop dialing, tear down listeners + iptables, set module.prop 🟡 "standby — waiting for network", stop battery-draining retries); route returns → auto-resume.
Reflect a standby state in the app Home status + module.prop (○ running / 🟡 standby / ● stopped / 💤 idle).
Files: daemon/cmd/sshcustomd/main.go, module/module.prop desc states, app Models.kt/HomeScreen.kt.
Risk control: I will not touch the SSH internet-path rules in ssh.iptables that you confirmed good on the v2.3.9 base (only add the hotspot FORWARD line, gated behind hotspot_sharing=true). Each fix is isolated; the pool refactor is the only large change and I'll keep the single-connection path as a fallback if pool size = 1.

E. Two decisions I need from you before building
Scope: do all four in one release (v2.4.0), or do you want them split (e.g., ship Fix 1+2+4 quickly, then the pool/speed refactor separately so it's easier to bisect if anything regresses)?
VPN-chain transport: v1.0.1 used the exact same OpenVPN-over-SOCKS path, so I'll keep it but remove the throughput-hurting flags and add the pool underneath. Confirm you don't want me to also pull in tun2socks (v1.0.1 started it but OpenVPN still used --socks-proxy directly, so it was effectively vestigial — I'd leave it out).
Say go (and your answers to 1–2 if you have a preference) and I'll build it. If you don't specify, I'll do all four in a single v2.4.0, keep the OpenVPN-over-SOCKS path, and ship it end-to-end (build, CI, release with downloadable ZIP+APK).

Now, your APK-vs-WebUI question — this is a genuinely important architectural call, so here's my honest analysis. Still holding, no build until you say go.

🧭 Should you dump the APK and go WebUI-only?
The single most important fact
The app provides ZERO runtime functionality. Every actual capability — SSH tunnel, OpenVPN chain, iptables, DNS, hotspot, reconnect, and the new standby — lives in the daemon + module scripts. The Kotlin app is purely a control surface: buttons that call scripts and a screen that polls status.

So dropping the APK loses nothing functional. It only changes how you press buttons and read status. That reframes the whole decision.

And here's the kicker
Every single bug that's been driving you crazy comes from the app's control surface, not the engine:

VPN chain stuck "Off" → libsu RootService binder being null/poisoned (app-only problem)
"idle when connected" module.prop → app/daemon write race (app side)
buttons disabled → Compose UI logic
"Stopped" flashes → Android killing the app process in background
The WebUI cannot have any of these, because it reads/controls the daemon directly over http://127.0.0.1:9190 — the exact same trust domain as the engine. This is precisely why v1.0.1 felt solid on your Poco F3: the daemon was the single source of truth, with no fragile Android IPC in the middle.

Trade-offs, honestly
✓ WebUI-only wins:

Kills the entire class of status-sync bugs by design — not by patching.
One language/build (Go + a webroot). No Android SDK, no Gradle, no R8, no APK signing, no 8 MB APK. CI gets ~2× faster.
Releases become one step — just reflash the ZIP. No more "reinstall APK and reflash ZIP."
No root-grant dialog for the app, no Doze/battery-optimization killing it, no background-process flakiness.
v1.0.1 already shipped WebUI-X/MMRL compatibility (safe-area insets, config.json, icon) — it works as a home-screen shortcut and feels app-like.
⚠️ WebUI-only costs:

You need a way to open it. On KernelSU (which you use) that's easy: the KSU/KSU-Next/MMRL manager has an "Open WebUI" button for the module, or WebUI-X Portable, or just a browser bookmark to 127.0.0.1:9190. On vanilla Magisk there's no built-in launcher (browser bookmark only) — but you're on KSU, so this is a non-issue.
No native notification / Quick-Settings tile / persistent foreground UI. (You don't need one — the daemon runs regardless of whether any UI is open.)
Slightly less "polished native feel" than the miuix Compose UI.
My recommendation
Go WebUI-only as the primary — it's the right call for a personal, reliability-first module on KernelSU, and it's literally the architecture that worked for you. But I'd do it in the smartest form:

Option A — Pure WebUI (what v1.0.1 did): ship a polished webroot served by the daemon; drop the APK entirely. Simplest, most robust, fastest to maintain.

Option B — Thin WebView wrapper (best of both): keep a tiny APK that's just a full-screen WebView pointing at 127.0.0.1:9190 (plus an optional foreground-service notification with Start/Stop). You get an app icon + launcher presence, but the UI/state is 100% the reliable daemon WebUI — so the libsu status bugs still vanish. ~200 lines of Kotlin instead of the whole Compose stack.

👉 If it were mine: Option A (pure WebUI). It's the cleanest, matches v1.0.1, and removes the most failure surface. If you like having an icon in your launcher, Option B gets you that without the bugs.

What this means for the v2.4.0 build
If you pick WebUI-only, the plan shifts (and actually gets cleaner):

The 4 fixes still happen, but Fix 1 (VPN-chain state) becomes trivial — the WebUI reads daemon state directly; no libsu at all.
I'd build a proper webroot (Home / VPN Chain / Logs / Settings / Profiles) and add the missing daemon /api/v1 endpoints (vpnchain start/stop/switch/status, settings write, profiles, log serving) — using v1.0.1's webui+API as the reference, modernized.
Module ships the webroot; no APK in CI.
The speed (SSH pool), hotspot (FORWARD ACCEPT), and standby (watchdog/route-aware daemon) fixes are identical regardless of UI choice.
So, your call before I build:

A = pure WebUI, drop APK (my pick), or
B = thin WebView-wrapper APK + WebUI, or
C = keep the full Compose app and just fix it (the v2.4.0 plan as originally written).
Tell me go + A/B/C and I'll ship all four fixes end-to-end (build → CI → downloadable release). If you just say go with no letter, I'll proceed with A (pure WebUI-only) since it best matches "what makes sense and works for you" and kills the recurring bugs at the root.

📐 WebUI-only (Option A) — full plan & analysis
1. What I learned about the MMRL WebUI-X ecosystem
WebUI-X Portable (MMRLApp/WebUI-X-Portable) — a standalone Android host app (on Google Play + GitHub) that opens any module's webroot/index.html as a full-screen app, on KernelSU, APatch, and Magisk. This is what gives a WebUI-only module a real "app" feel — icon, launcher entry, full screen — without us shipping an APK. Content rephrased for compliance.

WXU (MMRLApp/WXU) — an advanced .dex plugin system. It lets WebUI JS pull native modules via global.require("wx:fs"), wx:process, wx:pty, wx:pm, etc. — i.e. root filesystem/process/shell access straight from JavaScript. Powerful, but overkill for us (we don't need a custom dex; the daemon already does the privileged work). I'd skip it.

template-repository (MMRLApp/template-repository) — this is actually an MMRL repo template (a module catalog: json/modules.json, track.yaml, update.json, a GitHub Action sync_build_deploy.yml). Useful later if you want SSHCustom listed in an MMRL repo so it shows up in the MMRL app store with auto-updates. Not needed for the module itself.

The JS bridge & theming (the important part):

Module ships a webroot/ with index.html + config.json. v1.0.1 already has this.
config.json controls: title, icon, windowResize, backInterceptor ("javascript" = we handle back to close modals→Home→exit), and CSP. v1.0.1's is already correct.
WebUI-X injects safe-area insets as CSS variables (--window-inset-top/bottom/left/right) so content avoids the notch/nav bar — v1.0.1 already consumes these.
WebUI-X provides a system-theming CSS API that adapts to device system colors (Monet/Material You) per the official docs. So yes — Material You is achievable: the WebUI can pull the device's dynamic palette on WebUI-X hosts and fall back to a curated Material 3 palette elsewhere (browser/older host). This is progressive enhancement — looks great everywhere, true device-matched Material You on WebUI-X.
Control bridge: two options — (a) fetch() to the daemon at http://127.0.0.1:9190/api/... (works in any host/browser), or (b) KernelSU/WebUI-X ksu.exec() to run scripts directly (only inside a root WebView). v1.0.1 used (a), which is why it was portable and reliable. I'll keep (a).
2. What v1.0.1's WebUI actually is (and we're keeping it)
It's a single-file index.html (~1,540 lines) — a hand-built Material 3 dashboard, no framework, ~zero dependencies (instant load, tiny). It already has:

A bottom navigation bar (Home / Profiles / Runtime / VPN Chain / Settings), MD3 filled/tonal/text buttons, cards, MD3 switches, a bottom-sheet modal, toasts, pulsing status dot, page-enter animations, safe-area insets, mobile media queries, monospace log terminals.
A VPN Chain page already: location <select>, Start/Stop/Switch, status card, Windscribe exit IP card, and a chain log viewer.
A Runtime page with SSH Pool stats, streams, CPU/mem, route, resolver, and a log switcher (Core / Control / Action).
Talks to the daemon purely via fetch() to /api/v1/....
This is exactly the polished, mobile MD3 UI you remember. The plan keeps this structure and upgrades it (Material You theming + your current features), rather than rebuilding.

3. The core decision I'm proposing (needs your nod — Q1)
There are two daemons in play:

v1.0.1 daemon — has the SSH connection pool, network standby, full WebUI + /api/v1, but an older SSH/payload core.
v2 daemon (current) — has the carrier-critical payload/WebSocket SSH engine that actually gives you working internet on your carrier (the thing we so carefully preserved at the v2.3.9 base), plus dnsx, transparent proxy, reconnect.
➡️ My recommendation: keep the v2 daemon as the engine (so your internet keeps working), and port the good v1.0.1 parts into it — the SSH pool, the standby/route-awareness, the full /api/v1 surface, and the WebUI (webroot/ + embedded fallback). Then delete the Kotlin app. This preserves the working internet path and restores everything that made v1.0.1 good.

Pages: Home (status, Start/Stop/Restart, uptime, WAN + tunnel exit IP, live up/down speed, CPU/mem), VPN Chain (config select, Start/Stop/Switch, state, Windscribe IP, log), Logs (Core / Boot / Tool / OpenVPN selector — your requested dropdown), Settings (network mode, hotspot toggle, QUIC, DNS, autostart), Profiles (SSH profile CRUD), About/Standby state.

D. Module + CI:

I've analyzed deeply and here is the complete step-by-step /goal. Holding for your go — nothing built.

🎯 GOAL: SSHCustom-VPNChain v3.0.0 — WebUI-only, daemon-driven
North star
Delete the Android app entirely. The existing v2 Go daemon stays the engine (preserving your working carrier/payload internet path). A polished, mobile-first Material You WebUI — served by the daemon and shipped in webroot/ — becomes the only UI, working in the KSU manager WebView, WebUI-X (Portable/MMRL), and any browser. All four engine problems (VPN-chain state, hotspot, speed, standby) are fixed daemon-side, so the UI just reflects truth from one reliable source.

Key architecture decisions (final)
Engine: current v2 daemon, untouched on the SSH/payload/iptables internet path (your v2.3.9-based good internet). New capabilities are added, not swapped.
UI transport: WebUI talks to the daemon via fetch() → http://127.0.0.1:9190/api/v1/... only (portable across all hosts). Daemon gets permissive CORS so the KSU-manager WebView (which loads webroot/index.html as a static file, a different origin) can call the API.
Material You: progressive enhancement — consume WebUI-X's injected system-color (Monet) CSS variables when present; curated Material 3 dark/light palette as fallback; honor prefers-color-scheme.
tun2socks: dropped (OpenVPN-over-SOCKS doesn't need it — one less binary, smaller ZIP, less to break).
Version: v3.0.0. CI drops the APK job, ships ZIP-only.
PHASE 0 — Branch, scaffolding, version
0.1 New branch v3.0.0-webui from main.
0.2 Bump VERSION → 3.0.0; module.prop version=v3.0.0, versionCode=30000.
0.3 Add a GOAL.md capturing this plan (so it's tracked in-repo).
Acceptance: branch builds as-is before changes (baseline green).
PHASE 1 — Daemon API expansion (the backbone the WebUI needs)
The current daemon only exposes health, status, control, autostart, config, network/public-ip. The WebUI needs the full surface (modeled on v1.0.1's /api/v1). All in daemon/:

1.1 Add CORS middleware (Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *, methods, headers; handle OPTIONS) to all /api/v1 routes.
1.2 Extend StatusSnapshot (api/unix.go + HTTP /status) with: vpnchain_state (off/connecting/connected/error), vpnchain_exit_ip, vpnchain_location, standby (bool), pool_size, pool_healthy.
1.3 vpnchain endpoints: GET /api/v1/vpnchain/status, /locations, POST /start (body: location), /stop, /switch, GET /vpnchain/log. These call ovpn.service and read the marker/state — daemon owns the truth.
1.4 logs endpoints: GET /api/v1/logs/{core|boot|tool|openvpn} (tail N) + POST /api/v1/logs/{...}/clear. (Keeps your OpenVPN-in-logs requirement.)
1.5 profiles CRUD: GET/POST/DELETE /api/v1/profiles, POST /api/v1/profiles/active — persisted to profiles.json, written into settings.ini on activate (port the app's escaping logic server-side).
1.6 config patch: POST /api/v1/config for network_mode, hotspot_sharing, quic, dns_*, autostart, proxy_* → writes settings.ini, sets autostart marker.
1.7 network/public-ip already exists — extend to also return the VPN-chain exit IP (Windscribe) when chained.
Acceptance: every endpoint returns correct JSON via curl against a local run; go vet + build clean.
PHASE 2 — Engine fixes (daemon + scripts)
2.1 Speed — SSH connection pool. Introduce a pool of N SSH clients (N = channel_pool_size, default 8) in daemon/internal/ssh + wire startListeners/SOCKS/transparent to pick a healthy client per connection (round-robin/least-loaded). Keep single-connection fallback when N=1. Biggest throughput win.
2.2 Speed — SetNoDelay(true) on accepted SOCKS/transparent sockets (proxy/socks5.go, proxy/transparent.go).
2.3 Speed — OpenVPN flags. In ovpn.service, remove --tun-mtu 1280 --mssfix 1200 --sndbuf 524288 --rcvbuf 524288 (back to v1.0.1-style defaults). Keep the detached connect-watcher from v2.3.13.
2.4 Hotspot. In ssh.iptables hotspot block: keep ip_forward=1, add iptables -I FORWARD -j ACCEPT, drop route_localnet. Add matching cleanup in disable. (Matches the known-good v1.0.1 behavior.)
2.5 Network standby. Add route-awareness to the daemon's tunnel loop: poll default route (~10s). No route → STANDBY: stop dialing, tear down listeners + iptables, set standby=true, module.prop 🟡 "standby — waiting for network", stop battery-draining retries. Route returns → auto-resume. (Plus optional lightweight watchdog.sh belt-and-suspenders, like v1.0.1.)
2.6 module.prop states owned by daemon: ○ running / 🟡 standby / ● stopped / 💤 idle. Remove any conflicting script writes.
Acceptance: pool measurably increases parallel throughput; toggling airplane mode → 🟡 standby (no reconnect storm) → restore → auto-resume; hotspot client gets tunneled internet; chain reports connected + exit IP.
PHASE 3 — The WebUI (webroot/, the visible deliverable)
Single-file index.html (no framework → instant load, tiny), built on v1.0.1's structure, modernized:

3.1 Theming: Material 3 token system; Material You via WebUI-X system-color variables (confirm exact names from mmrl.dev docs at build) with curated dark/light fallback; prefers-color-scheme; safe-area insets (--window-inset-*).
3.2 Shell: bottom navigation bar, top app bar, page-transition animations, toasts, bottom-sheet editors, pulsing status dot, pull-to-refresh, live polling of /api/v1/status.
3.3 Pages (my intuition):
Home — status (running/standby/stopped/idle), Start/Stop/Restart (always clickable), uptime, WAN IP + tunnel exit IP, live ↑/↓ speed, CPU/mem, latency, SSH pool health.
VPN Chain — location dropdown, Start/Stop/Switch, state, Windscribe exit IP, chain log.
Logs — selector Core / Boot / Tool / OpenVPN + clear + autoscroll (your requirement, kept).
Settings — network mode, hotspot toggle, QUIC, DNS, autostart.
Profiles — SSH profile CRUD + activate.
About — version, links, standby/route info.
3.4 Assets: config.json (title/icon/insets/back-handling/CSP — ported from v1.0.1), icon.png, favicon.svg.
3.5 Portability: verify it works (a) loaded as static file by KSU manager (fetch → :9190 via CORS), (b) WebUI-X Portable, (c) browser at :9190 (same-origin). Back-button: close modal → Home → exit.
Acceptance: every control/round-trips to the daemon; renders cleanly on a phone viewport; Monet accent on WebUI-X, dark fallback elsewhere.
PHASE 4 — Module packaging + app removal
4.1 Daemon embeds webroot/index.html as fallback (port v1.0.1's webui embed pattern); ships on-disk webroot/ so it's hot-editable + WebUI-X-discoverable.
4.2 customize.sh/update-binary install webroot/ into the module; module.prop description default 💤 idle.
4.3 Delete the entire app/ Kotlin module.
Acceptance: ZIP contains bin/, scripts/, webroot/, module.prop, service.sh, settings.ini; flashes clean.
PHASE 5 — CI/CD
5.1 Remove build-apk job + APK release asset from .github/workflows/build.yml.
5.2 Build step copies webroot/index.html→internal/webui/ before go build (embed), builds daemon (+ OpenVPN; no tun2socks), packages ZIP, creates the GitHub release.
5.3 Fix the release-notes template (currently stale text) to reflect v3.0.0.
Acceptance: tag push → green CI → release with one downloadable ZIP.
PHASE 6 — Verify, ship
6.1 Local: go vet ./..., cross-build GOOS=android GOARCH=arm64, sh -n all scripts, HTML/JSON sanity, curl every endpoint against a local daemon run.
6.2 Commit, tag v3.0.0, push branch+tag, merge to main, monitor CI, verify the release ZIP is downloadable.
6.3 Report back: what changed, how to flash, how to open the WebUI on KSU/WebUI-X, and the known-limits (e.g., OpenVPN-over-SSH has inherent overhead even with the pool).
Risk controls
Internet path is sacred: I do not touch the SSH redirect/payload rules in ssh.iptables except adding the gated hotspot FORWARD ACCEPT. Your v2.3.9-based good internet stays intact.
Pool is the only large refactor: falls back to single-connection if N=1, so it can't make things worse than today.
Each phase is independently verifiable, so if anything regresses we can bisect by phase.
One thing I'll confirm during build (not a blocker)
Exact WebUI-X Monet CSS variable names — the mmrl.dev docs page is JS-rendered and didn't extract cleanly; I'll pull the doc markdown from the MMRL docs repo at build time and wire the real names, with the dark fallback guaranteeing it looks right regardless.
